CookSim · reference · the suite now running, case by case

The fifty benchmark cases

This is the suite the formal sweep is executing right now, opened up one card per case. Each card says which kitchen the episode is cooked in and what the order is; then, in the order they are scheduled, every mistake the engine will force the cook to make, every question the cook will ask out loud, every standing request it will hand over and every change of plan it will announce — each with the moment that makes it fire, what it does to the world, and how an assistant is credited for it. At the foot of every card is what actually happened when that case was played once through, so the difference between a sub-case that is designed and a sub-case that fires is visible per case rather than as an aggregate.

Provenance suite bench/v5/cases_composite_v5.json suite sha256 5dd1e078214325fc85f6a62dd7418651b708b0362c13e5e638a89ce2a4b74db6 engine_rev 0907dedb12 — the rev the formal run is executing kitchens built in memory by exp_interact.build_episode(), the function the rollout calls, with each case's own inject flag (COOKSIM_GRID = tmp/grid.json) firing rules docs/FIRING_AND_GRADING.md and the code it was read out of rubrics tools/qa_v3_lib.py (RUBRIC, QA_SCORE) verification episodes tmp/suite_r5d/llm2 — the whole suite played once, baseline cook persona, Gemini-Flash LLM cook, SILENT assistant seat, scheduler v5, pace tight page generated 2026-09-21 by tools/build_cases_page.py

What is in the suite

50cases
260sub-cases
140planted mistakes
76of those ruinous
88questions
29standing requests
3plan changes
10kitchens

Fifty cases, 260 sub-cases. A case is one episode of one dish on one kitchen map; a sub-case is one testable event inside it. The error load rises with the tier — 2 at easy, 2 at medium, 3 at hard, 4 at expert, 3 at master — and falls back at master because the burrito's five components leave less room for a fourth mistake. 76 of the 140 mistakes are marked ruinous: the dish is rejected at the pass if the mistake stands. Each tier is five cases on a medium map and five on a hard map, the same dish on both.

The mistakes, by the type that actually runs

Two designed labels are realized by a different function: 17 sub-cases designed add_foreign run misidentify_assemble (replace the due item rather than add a spare) and 10 designed omit_subgoal run premature_serve (hand the plate in early rather than skip a step). Neither realized name is one the assistant is offered, so for every one of the 140 the name that earns credit is the designed one. Both are shown on the cards.

forget_next_step19

The questions, by family

Q9 · next_step, ready_in20
Q3 · elapsed, past_done20
Q5 · empty_boards, missing14
Q4 · happened9
Q8 · assistant_said9
Q2 · count8
Q1 · order5
Q7 · after_ref3

Plus 29 standing requests (family Q10, “give me a shout the moment the X is done”) and 3 announced plan changes — the one family whose correct assistant behaviour is to say nothing, so every objection to one is a false alarm by construction.

How to read a card

The sub-cases are listed in the order the suite schedules them, which is the order they are meant to happen in. None of them is a tick: an anchor is a progress floor — a state of the dish — and the scheduler re-checks every pending sub-case against the live kitchen on every tick, so the same case runs at whatever pace the cook works at. Two resources are arbitrated separately: mistakes and plan changes take the cook's body, one at a time with a ten-tick gap between them; questions and requests take its voice, one at a time, never inside an error takeover and never on the tick the dish goes to the pass. The full rules are on the firing and grading page; the cards quote the part of them that applies.

The window on a mistake is what a detection is graded against: it opens at the first observable deviated act and closes at the tick the harm lands. The figure on the card is the median measured over the verification run for that realized type, with its range; where the case fired, that instance's own window is given in the evidence strip at the foot of the card. The suite file also carries a window per sub-case, and the cards deliberately do not show it: it is a flat 32 ticks for every type but the walk-away (49), left over from the scripted replay the placements were screened on, and grading stopped reading it when the live window rule landed. The difference is not cosmetic — the real median for an add_foreign that goes on a finished plate is 7.5 ticks, and an API assistant's own reply takes 4 to 8.

0 · mistake

A raw tomato goes on the plate while no tomato is chopped yetruinous

type
serve_raw
the cook plates an ingredient straight from its crate when the recipe requires it cooked or chopped, and never starts cooking it either
item
tomato
kwargs {"component": "tomato"}
fires when
The tomato must still be genuinely raw, and no unit of it may be finishing inside the block's own walk to the crate — a raw one must not be scooped past a good one that is arriving. Then the shared force gate: the dish is under way, the plate is neither complete nor already queued for the pass. The tomato must not be on the plate in any state yet, the plate must not be full, and the recipe must not plate the tomato raw anyway.
forced block
the tomato's assembly step becomes a raw scoop, and both its prep and its ignition are deleted from the plan — four commands with the plate fetch: go to the plates · pick up · go to the tomato source · scoop.
as spliced in the verification run: go to the plates · pick up · go to the tomato source · scoop
harm lands
a unit of the tomato reaches the plate after the injection in a state the recipe does not want. The cook gets its hands back on max hold, plate_committed.
catch window
median 11.0 ticks for serve_raw over the 7 that fired in the verification run (range 10–17); 11 ticks in this case. The assistant must name serve_raw inside it; mis_prep, repeat_step count as aliases, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

1 · question · Q5

“How many cutting boards are free right now?”
kind
empty_boards:board — 1 of 2 boards empty
can be asked
Any time the progress floor is met and the voice slot is free — no extra precondition of its own. Beyond that the voice slot must be free and quiet, no error takeover may be live, and the serve ceiling must not have closed it.
truth
Computed against the live kitchen at the moment of the ask and again at the moment of the answer — never read from a reference plan. An answer that was true when it was given is not wrong. On the clean scripted trace this anchor the answer was {"boards": 2, "empty": 1, "empty_at": [[0, 1]], "answer": "1"}.
judged on
the number of free boards against live board occupancy. Note that the rubric the judge is handed for this family is written for the missing items kind — “grade the SET” — and not for a count; 13 of the suite's 14 Q5 instances are this counting kind.

An extra off-recipe item goes on the finished plateruinous

type
add_foreign
an ingredient the recipe never asked for is scooped onto the plate in addition to everything that belongs there
item
lettuce
kwargs {"before": "serve", "wrong": "lettuce"}
fires when
The dish must be under way and then finished — the plate holds every goal item, or the cook's own serve is already queued. The extra goes onto a plate the cook believes is done. The plate must have room (a full plate of five cannot take a scoop) and the map must have a lettuce crate.
Stated progress floor: at least 2 items already on the plate.
forced block
a walk to the lettuce crate and a scoop, inserted before the target step. Because the block scoops, the engine walks the cook's hands through it first and inserts the plate fetch immediately before the walk that leads to that scoop.
as spliced in the verification run: go to the lettuce source · scoop
harm lands
a new unit of the lettuce reaches the plate after the injection. The cook gets its hands back on max hold, plate_committed.
catch window
median 7.5 ticks for add_foreign over the 16 that fired in the verification run (range 3–11); 6 ticks in this case. The assistant must name add_foreign inside it; repeat_step counts as an alias, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

1 · mistake

Stalls after fetch_platenot ruinous

type
forget_next_step
the cook loses its plan and stands there doing nothing; there is no trace on the plate, so the only way to catch it is to be watching
fires when
The anchor is a progress floor: the plate is in the cook's hands. Two standing refusals then apply. A forget never forgets the serve — the moment is refused whenever every goal item is on the plate, the plate is full, or the cook's own next command is the serve. And no food may reach smoke inside the freeze: the check looks 40 ticks ahead (the 35-tick stall plus the 5 the cook needs to walk back), because freezing beside food that will smoke is a walk-away, not a forget.
forced block
one command — idle 35. The cook stands still for the whole freeze (FORGET_FREEZE_TICKS = 35, sized as the harness's 30-tick latency cap plus the 5 ticks the cook needs to walk back) and then picks its own plan up again.
as spliced in the verification run: idle 35
harm lands
the stall runs to its end. The cook gets its hands back on the block has run out (deviated_block_executed).
catch window
median 37.0 ticks for forget_next_step over the 18 that fired in the verification run (range 37–49); 37 ticks in this case. The assistant must name forget_next_step inside it; repeat_step counts as an alias, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

An off-recipe item goes into the cheese's slotruinous

type
add_foreign — designed under this label but realized by misidentify_assemble, which is not a name the assistant is offered. The name that earns credit is add_foreign.
the wrong thing is scooped into the slot where a recipe item should have gone — nothing extra appears, something correct is simply missing and something wrong is in its place
item
cheese (the wrong thing scooped or prepared: onion)
kwargs {"component": "cheese", "wrong": "onion"}
fires when
No unit of the cheese may be finishing on this very tick; then the force gate — the dish under way, the plate neither complete nor queued for the pass. The cheese's own slot must still be open (a correctly plated cheese means the moment has passed), the plate must have room, and the map must have an onion crate. Nothing is asked of the cook's own plan: the block fetches the plate itself.
Stated progress floor: prep tomato x1; at least 1 item already on the plate.
forced block
the cheese's own assembly step is replaced by a walk to the onion crate and a scoop, with the plate fetch inserted ahead of it — typically go to the plates · pick up · go to the onion source · scoop.
as spliced in the verification run: go to the onion source · scoop
harm lands
a new unit of the onion reaches the plate after the injection. The cook gets its hands back on max hold, plate_committed.

0 · question · Q9

“What is my next step?”
kind
next_step:plan — next fetch_plate, then None
can be asked
Never while a forget_next_step is pending or live — the forgetful cook asking its next step would be answered with its own repair. Progress floor: prep tomato x1. Beyond that the voice slot must be free and quiet, no error takeover may be live, and the serve ceiling must not have closed it.
truth
Computed against the live kitchen at the moment of the ask and again at the moment of the answer — never read from a reference plan. An answer that was true when it was given is not wrong. On the clean scripted trace this anchor the answer was {"recipe": ["tomato", "cheese"], "plate": [], "plate_where": null, "remaining": ["tomato", "cheese"], "source": "world", "next_subgoal": "fetch_plate", "then": null, "also_acceptable": [], "stages": {"tomato": ["ready"], ….
judged on
the named next step must be the truth's own next step or one of its also_acceptable alternatives; a step the order no longer needs, or one that cannot be done yet, is incorrect.

1 · mistake

Does the tomato prep a second timenot ruinous

type
repeat_step
the cook loses track of having already done a step and goes back to do it again
item
tomato
kwargs {"sid": "prep(tomato)", "at": "assemble(tomato)"}
fires when
The at anchor is a progress floor: the tomato must be correctly on the plate — without it a prep repeat fired the tick its component first entered a station, which is a consecutive re-do and not a memory slip. This is a prep repeat — the tomato is fetched and processed a second time, which costs time rather than the dish. It needs a free station to do it in: a block whose walk to an empty board has no destination is refused and the sub-case waits. The item must also not be one whose second unit reads as legitimate — a second bun is a top bun, not a duplicate.
Stated progress floor: prep tomato x1; at least 1 item already on the plate.
forced block
the template step is simply run again now. For a chopped item with no second prepared unit left, the block parks the dish plate, makes the duplicate itself, and picks the same plate back up so the duplicate lands on the dish and not on a scratch plate.
as spliced in the verification run: go to the empty counter · put down · go to the tomato source · pick up · go to the empty cutting board · put down · chop
harm lands
one plate build holds more units of the tomato than the recipe needs (an assemble repeat), or more units than needed have entered processing (a prep repeat). The cook gets its hands back on the block has run out (deviated_block_executed).
catch window
median 31.5 ticks for repeat_step over the 12 that fired in the verification run (range 2–49); 28 ticks in this case. The assistant must name repeat_step inside it; add_foreign counts as an alias, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

0 · mistake

Skips the tomato although it is ready and plates the cheese insteadruinous

type
omit_subgoal
the cook believes a required step is already done, skips it and plates the next item instead, so the skipped one is simply missing from the dish
item
tomato
kwargs {"sid": "assemble(tomato)"}
fires when
The force gate — the dish under way, the plate neither complete nor queued for the pass. The tomato's slot must not already be correctly filled, the plate must have room, and, decisively, the next recipe item must be ready, because the omission is realized by plating that next item instead.
Stated progress floor: prep tomato x1.
forced block
the short omission: put the plate in hand, skip the tomato entirely, plate the next recipe item instead, and hand the hands back.
as spliced in the verification run: go to the plates · pick up · go to the cheese source · scoop
harm lands
an item that comes after the tomato is plated while the tomato is still missing, or the short plate is served. The cook gets its hands back on the block has run out (deviated_block_executed).
catch window
median 17.0 ticks for omit_subgoal over the 9 that fired in the verification run (range 11–30); 11 ticks in this case. The assistant must name omit_subgoal inside it; wrong_order, repeat_step count as aliases, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

1 · mistake

Puts a second tomato on the platenot ruinous

type
repeat_step
the cook loses track of having already done a step and goes back to do it again
item
tomato
kwargs {"sid": "assemble(tomato)", "at": "assemble(tomato)"}
fires when
The at anchor is a progress floor: the tomato must be correctly on the plate — without it a prep repeat fired the tick its component first entered a station, which is a consecutive re-do and not a memory slip. This is an assemble repeat: the dish must be under way with at least one correct unit of the tomato already plated. A complete plate does not bar the duplicate — that is where it is most visible as one — only a full plate of five does. The duplicate must be scoopable: a raw one comes from its crate, a chopped one the block chops itself, a cooked one needs a second cooked unit the kitchen can supply. The item must also not be one whose second unit reads as legitimate — a second bun is a top bun, not a duplicate.
Stated progress floor: prep tomato x1; at least 1 item already on the plate.
forced block
the template step is simply run again now. For a chopped item with no second prepared unit left, the block parks the dish plate, makes the duplicate itself, and picks the same plate back up so the duplicate lands on the dish and not on a scratch plate.
as spliced in the verification run: go to the empty counter · put down · go to the tomato source · pick up · go to the empty cutting board · put down · chop · go to the counter with the plate · pick up · go to the cutting board with the tomato · scoop
harm lands
one plate build holds more units of the tomato than the recipe needs (an assemble repeat), or more units than needed have entered processing (a prep repeat). The cook gets its hands back on the block has run out (deviated_block_executed).
catch window
median 31.5 ticks for repeat_step over the 12 that fired in the verification run (range 2–49); 49 ticks in this case. The assistant must name repeat_step inside it; add_foreign counts as an alias, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

forget_next_step

fires when
The anchor is a progress floor: the tomato is in processing. Two standing refusals then apply. A forget never forgets the serve — the moment is refused whenever every goal item is on the plate, the plate is full, or the cook's own next command is the serve. And no food may reach smoke inside the freeze: the check looks 40 ticks ahead (the 35-tick stall plus the 5 the cook needs to walk back), because freezing beside food that will smoke is a walk-away, not a forget.

0 · mistake

Plates the cheese before the bunruinous

type
wrong_order
on a dish whose assembly order is strict, the cook plates the right items in the wrong order
item
cheese — the item plated too early, before the bun
kwargs {"a": "bun", "b": "cheese"}
fires when
This dish is order-strict, which is the only kind this type is ever placed on. No unit of the bun — the item that should have gone first — may be finishing on this very tick; then the force gate. Neither the bun nor the cheese may already be correctly plated, and the cheese (the item that will go down too early) must be ready, or be a chopped item the block can make itself. Only the item plated too early has to exist: requiring the bun as well starved every live run.
forced block
the two assembly steps are swapped. If the cheese is a chopped item and no chopped unit exists, the block parks the plate, chops the cheese itself, retrieves the same plate and scoops the cheese before the bun. While the block runs, the bun is held back from smoking.
as spliced in the verification run: go to the plates · pick up · go to the cheese source · scoop · go to the bun source · scoop
harm lands
some plate build received the cheese before the bun, or holds the cheese with no bun under it. The cook gets its hands back on the whole block has run — deliberately not at the plate commit, because releasing on the first scoop turned the swap into an omission.
catch window
median 40.0 ticks for wrong_order over the 8 that fired in the verification run (range 18–46); 22 ticks in this case. The assistant must name wrong_order inside it; omit_subgoal, repeat_step count as aliases, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

1 · question · Q9

“What is my next step?”
kind
next_step:plan — next fetch_plate, then None
can be asked
Never while a forget_next_step is pending or live — the forgetful cook asking its next step would be answered with its own repair. Progress floor: prep meat x1. Beyond that the voice slot must be free and quiet, no error takeover may be live, and the serve ceiling must not have closed it.
truth
Computed against the live kitchen at the moment of the ask and again at the moment of the answer — never read from a reference plan. An answer that was true when it was given is not wrong. On the clean scripted trace this anchor the answer was {"recipe": ["bun", "meat", "cheese"], "plate": [], "plate_where": null, "remaining": ["bun", "meat", "cheese"], "source": "world", "next_subgoal": "fetch_plate", "then": null, "also_acceptable": [], "stages": {"bun": ["n ….
judged on
the named next step must be the truth's own next step or one of its also_acceptable alternatives; a step the order no longer needs, or one that cannot be done yet, is incorrect.

2 · mistake

Walks away from the meat near donenot ruinous

type
leave_unattended
the cook walks off to other business while food that is about to be ready sits on a lit station, and the food goes through done, grace, smoke and fire behind its back
item
meat
kwargs {"component": "meat", "loops": 2}
fires when
An anchor floor first (the prep the anchor names is reached and at least as many items are plated). Then the food itself: the meat must be near done — from 6 ticks before it finishes to 20 ticks after, an asymmetric window, and never on food that is already smoking. No other food may be lost by the walk (anything elsewhere that would smoke inside the circuit plus the walk back bars it), and it never fires within 3 ticks of a standing request about the same food — a cook that has just handed the watch to the assistant is not walking away negligently.
forced block
busywork drawn from the recipe itself — every other component's crate, then the plates and the serving pass, each followed by an idle of about eight ticks, the whole circuit walked twice. The circuit is rotated, reversed and jittered per sub-case from a stable seed so it does not read as a script. If the cook is holding the half-built plate, the block parks it first.
as spliced in the verification run: go to the plates · idle 5 · go to the serving pass · idle 8 · go to the plates · idle 10 · go to the serving pass · idle 9
harm lands
a busywork walk has finished and the meat's own food has begun to smoke. The cook gets its hands back on max hold at the smoke (smoke_committed) — the rescue is handed back at once.
catch window
median 29.0 ticks for leave_unattended over the 12 that fired in the verification run (range 5–32); 32 ticks in this case. The assistant must name leave_unattended inside it; forget_next_step, omit_subgoal count as aliases, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

3 · question · Q8

“What did you tell me about the pan earlier?”
kind
assistant_said:pan — asked at 53% of the plan (live-only truth)
can be asked
Progress floor only; the screen refuses it before half the plan, when there is little to remember. Progress floor: prep meat x1. Beyond that the voice slot must be free and quiet, no error takeover may be live, and the serve ceiling must not have closed it.
truth
Computed against the live kitchen at the moment of the ask and again at the moment of the answer — never read from a reference plan. An answer that was true when it was given is not wrong. On the clean scripted trace this anchor the answer was {"topic": "pan", "said": null, "assistant_lines_so_far": 0, "answer": "nothing \u2014 the assistant has not mentioned the pan"}.
judged on
the assistant must recall its own earlier line in substance; if it never said anything, the only correct answer is that it did not.

4 · announced change of plan

The cook changes its mind about the order and says so: “add some fresh lettuce on top before serving”. It is not a mistake and the correct assistant behaviour is to leave it alone — every objection here is a false alarm by construction.not an error

what the cook is told
To begin its next line with “I want to modify the original recipe:” and state the change in one sentence — add some fresh lettuce on top before serving — then plan and do it itself before serving, and not to accept being told it is a mistake.
fires when
Like a question: a progress floor (prep meat x1) and the serve ceiling — announcing a change on the serve tick is worthless. It takes the body slot even though nothing is spliced into the cook's queue.
what it changes
The engine rewrites the open order and the recipe book: {"ingredient": "lettuce", "state": "raw"}. Everything that asks what the dish needs then reads the new answer — the success check at the pass, the anchors of later mistakes, and the truth of every later question.
judged on
The assistant is scored by what it does not do: objecting is a false alarm. The cook is judged from the world — the served plate must hold the goal's full count of the announced item — and the sub-case releases the moment the cook announces it, or after 12 ticks as not_announced.

2 · mistake

Carries the plate to the pass with only the bun (and what came before) on itruinous

type
omit_subgoal — designed under this label but realized by premature_serve, which is not a name the assistant is offered. The name that earns credit is omit_subgoal.
the cook decides the dish is finished when it is not, abandons the rest and carries an incomplete plate to the pass
fires when
The force gate, and then a position floor: as many units on the plate as the recipe wants up to and including the item named by assemble(bun), whatever those units are. An early serve is premature by how far along the dish is, not by whether the cook plated in the template's order. An empty plate cannot be served.
Stated progress floor: at least 1 item already on the plate.
forced block
put the plate in hand, walk to the serving pass and serve. Because the block ends in the serve, it releases at the serve — that is, never before the harm has landed.
as spliced in the verification run: go to the serving pass · serve
harm lands
the plate is served with units missing, counted as units and not as a set of names. The cook gets its hands back on nothing: the block ends in the serve, which ends the episode.
catch window
median 7.0 ticks for premature_serve over the 8 that fired in the verification run (range 5–18); 11 ticks in this case. The assistant must name omit_subgoal inside it; wrong_order, repeat_step count as aliases, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

3 · announced change of plan

The cook changes its mind about the order and says so: “add some chopped onion on top before serving”. It is not a mistake and the correct assistant behaviour is to leave it alone — every objection here is a false alarm by construction.not an error

what the cook is told
To begin its next line with “I want to modify the original recipe:” and state the change in one sentence — add some chopped onion on top before serving — then plan and do it itself before serving, and not to accept being told it is a mistake.
fires when
Like a question: a progress floor (prep meat x1) and the serve ceiling — announcing a change on the serve tick is worthless. It takes the body slot even though nothing is spliced into the cook's queue.
what it changes
The engine rewrites the open order and the recipe book: {"ingredient": "onion", "state": "raw"}. Everything that asks what the dish needs then reads the new answer — the success check at the pass, the anchors of later mistakes, and the truth of every later question.
judged on
The assistant is scored by what it does not do: objecting is a false alarm. The cook is judged from the world — the served plate must hold the goal's full count of the announced item — and the sub-case releases the moment the cook announces it, or after 12 ticks as not_announced.

3 · mistake

Stalls after fetch_platenot ruinous

type
forget_next_step
the cook loses its plan and stands there doing nothing; there is no trace on the plate, so the only way to catch it is to be watching
fires when
The anchor is a progress floor: the plate is in the cook's hands. Two standing refusals then apply. A forget never forgets the serve — the moment is refused whenever every goal item is on the plate, the plate is full, or the cook's own next command is the serve. And no food may reach smoke inside the freeze: the check looks 40 ticks ahead (the 35-tick stall plus the 5 the cook needs to walk back), because freezing beside food that will smoke is a walk-away, not a forget.
forced block
one command — idle 35. The cook stands still for the whole freeze (FORGET_FREEZE_TICKS = 35, sized as the harness's 30-tick latency cap plus the 5 ticks the cook needs to walk back) and then picks its own plan up again.
as spliced in the verification run: idle 35
harm lands
the stall runs to its end. The cook gets its hands back on the block has run out (deviated_block_executed).

5 · announced change of plan

The cook changes its mind about the order and says so: “add some fresh lettuce on top before serving”. It is not a mistake and the correct assistant behaviour is to leave it alone — every objection here is a false alarm by construction.not an error

what the cook is told
To begin its next line with “I want to modify the original recipe:” and state the change in one sentence — add some fresh lettuce on top before serving — then plan and do it itself before serving, and not to accept being told it is a mistake.
fires when
Like a question: a progress floor (prep potato x2) and the serve ceiling — announcing a change on the serve tick is worthless. It takes the body slot even though nothing is spliced into the cook's queue.
what it changes
The engine rewrites the open order and the recipe book: {"ingredient": "lettuce", "state": "raw"}. Everything that asks what the dish needs then reads the new answer — the success check at the pass, the anchors of later mistakes, and the truth of every later question.
judged on
The assistant is scored by what it does not do: objecting is a false alarm. The cook is judged from the world — the served plate must hold the goal's full count of the announced item — and the sub-case releases the moment the cook announces it, or after 12 ticks as not_announced.

6 · mistake

Does the potato prep a second timenot ruinous

type
repeat_step
the cook loses track of having already done a step and goes back to do it again
item
potato
kwargs {"sid": "prep(potato)", "at": "assemble(potato)"}
fires when
The at anchor is a progress floor: the potato must be correctly on the plate — without it a prep repeat fired the tick its component first entered a station, which is a consecutive re-do and not a memory slip. This is a prep repeat — the potato is fetched and processed a second time, which costs time rather than the dish. It needs a free station to do it in: a block whose walk to an empty board has no destination is refused and the sub-case waits. The item must also not be one whose second unit reads as legitimate — a second bun is a top bun, not a duplicate.
Stated progress floor: prep potato x1; at least 2 items already on the plate.
forced block
the template step is simply run again now. For a chopped item with no second prepared unit left, the block parks the dish plate, makes the duplicate itself, and picks the same plate back up so the duplicate lands on the dish and not on a scratch plate.
as spliced in the verification run: go to the empty counter · put down · go to the potato source · pick up · go to the empty cutting board · put down · chop · pick up · go to the empty fryer · put down
harm lands
one plate build holds more units of the potato than the recipe needs (an assemble repeat), or more units than needed have entered processing (a prep repeat). The cook gets its hands back on the block has run out (deviated_block_executed).
catch window
median 31.5 ticks for repeat_step over the 12 that fired in the verification run (range 2–49); 37 ticks in this case. The assistant must name repeat_step inside it; add_foreign counts as an alias, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

Preps the wrong thing for the potatonot ruinous

type
mis_prep

item
potato (the wrong thing scooped or prepared: lettuce)
kwargs {"component": "potato", "wrong": "lettuce"}
fires when
The one moment test is the cook's own reach: its current or next command names the potato or its crate, or it is already holding a raw potato. The potato must not be fully prepped already, and the map must have a lettuce crate. This type waits out every smoke — it is not one of the types whose smoke ban can be narrowed.
forced block
a whole prep block for the lettuce, built out of steps this kitchen allows: fetch it, carry it to a free board or the station the real component would have used, and process it there. 2 · mistake

Stalls after fetch_platenot ruinous

type
forget_next_step
the cook loses its plan and stands there doing nothing; there is no trace on the plate, so the only way to catch it is to be watching
fires when
The anchor is a progress floor: the plate is in the cook's hands. Two standing refusals then apply. A forget never forgets the serve — the moment is refused whenever every goal item is on the plate, the plate is full, or the cook's own next command is the serve. And no food may reach smoke inside the freeze: the check looks 40 ticks ahead (the 35-tick stall plus the 5 the cook needs to walk back), because freezing beside food that will smoke is a walk-away, not a forget.
forced block
one command — idle 35. The cook stands still for the whole freeze (FORGET_FREEZE_TICKS = 35, sized as the harness's 30-tick latency cap plus the 5 ticks the cook needs to walk back) and then picks its own plan up again.
harm lands
the stall runs to its end. The cook gets its hands back on the block has run out (deviated_block_executed).
catch window
median 37.0 ticks for forget_next_step over the 18 that fired in the verification run (range 37–49). The assistant must name forget_next_step inside it; repeat_step counts as an alias, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

item

fires when

forced block

harm lands

forced block
a walk to the noodles crate and a scoop, inserted before the target step. Because the block scoops, the engine walks the cook's hands through it first and inserts the plate fetch immediately before the walk that leads to that scoop.
harm lands
a new unit of the noodles reaches the plate after the injection. The cook gets its hands back on max hold, plate_committed.
catch window
median 7.5 ticks for add_foreign over the 16 that fired in the verification run (range 3–11). The assistant must name add_foreign inside it; repeat_step counts as an alias, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

0 · mistake

Stalls after prep(steak)not ruinous

type
forget_next_step
the cook loses its plan and stands there doing nothing; there is no trace on the plate, so the only way to catch it is to be watching
fires when
The anchor is a progress floor: the steak is in processing. Two standing refusals then apply. A forget never forgets the serve — the moment is refused whenever every goal item is on the plate, the plate is full, or the cook's own next command is the serve. And no food may reach smoke inside the freeze: the check looks 40 ticks ahead (the 35-tick stall plus the 5 the cook needs to walk back), because freezing beside food that will smoke is a walk-away, not a forget.
forced block
one command — idle 35. The cook stands still for the whole freeze (FORGET_FREEZE_TICKS = 35, sized as the harness's 30-tick latency cap plus the 5 ticks the cook needs to walk back) and then picks its own plan up again.
as spliced in the verification run: idle 35
harm lands
the stall runs to its end. The cook gets its hands back on the block has run out (deviated_block_executed).
catch window
median 37.0 ticks for forget_next_step over the 18 that fired in the verification run (range 37–49); 37 ticks in this case. The assistant must name forget_next_step inside it; repeat_step counts as an alias, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

1 · mistake

Preps the wrong thing for the potatonot ruinous

type
mis_prep
the wrong ingredient is fetched and prepared at the station the right one should have used, so the real component is never made
item
potato (the wrong thing scooped or prepared: cheese)
kwargs {"component": "potato", "wrong": "cheese"}
fires when
The one moment test is the cook's own reach: its current or next command names the potato or its crate, or it is already holding a raw potato. The potato must not be fully prepped already, and the map must have a cheese crate. This type waits out every smoke — it is not one of the types whose smoke ban can be narrowed.
forced block
a whole prep block for the cheese, built out of steps this kitchen allows: fetch it, carry it to a free board or the station the real component would have used, and process it there. If the cook is already holding something, the block parks it first; an orphaned ignition of the real component is rewritten or dropped.
as spliced in the verification run: go to the cheese source · pick up · go to the empty cutting board · put down · chop
harm lands
the cheese has been processed. The cook gets its hands back on the block has run out (deviated_block_executed).
catch window
median 23.0 ticks for mis_prep over the 13 that fired in the verification run (range 16–37); 37 ticks in this case. The assistant must name mis_prep inside it; repeat_step, serve_raw count as aliases, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

2 · question · Q9

“What is my next step?”
kind
next_step:plan — next prep(potato), then ignite(steak)
can be asked
Never while a forget_next_step is pending or live — the forgetful cook asking its next step would be answered with its own repair. Progress floor: prep steak x1. Beyond that the voice slot must be free and quiet, no error takeover may be live, and the serve ceiling must not have closed it.
truth
Computed against the live kitchen at the moment of the ask and again at the moment of the answer — never read from a reference plan. An answer that was true when it was given is not wrong. judged on
the named next step must be the truth's own next step or one of its also_acceptable alternatives; a step the order no longer needs, or one that cannot be done yet, is incorrect.

3 · mistake

Walks away from the steak near donenot ruinous

type
leave_unattended
the cook walks off to other business while food that is about to be ready sits on a lit station, and the food goes through done, grace, smoke and fire behind its back
item
steak
kwargs {"component": "steak", "loops": 2}
fires when
An anchor floor first (the prep the anchor names is reached and at least as many items are plated). Then the food itself: the steak must be near done — from 6 ticks before it finishes to 20 ticks after, an asymmetric window, and never on food that is already smoking. No other food may be lost by the walk (anything elsewhere that would smoke inside the circuit plus the walk back bars it), and it never fires within 3 ticks of a standing request about the same food — a cook that has just handed the watch to the assistant is not walking away negligently.
forced block
busywork drawn from the recipe itself — every other component's crate, then the plates and the serving pass, each followed by an idle of about eight ticks, the whole circuit walked twice. The circuit is rotated, reversed and jittered per sub-case from a stable seed so it does not read as a script. If the cook is holding the half-built plate, the block parks it first.
harm lands
a busywork walk has finished and the steak's own food has begun to smoke. The cook gets its hands back on max hold at the smoke (smoke_committed) — the rescue is handed back at once.
catch window
median 29.0 ticks for leave_unattended over the 12 that fired in the verification run (range 5–32). The assistant must name leave_unattended inside it; forget_next_step, omit_subgoal count as aliases, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

can be asked

truth
Computed against the live kitchen at the moment of the ask and again at the moment of the answer — never read from a reference plan. An answer that was true when it was given is not wrong. On the clean scripted trace this anchor the answer was {"done_on_heat": [], "answer": "no"}.
judged on
the named station must match and any stated tick count must be within 5 ticks of the truth.

5 · mistake

Carries the plate to the pass with only the steak (and what came before) on itruinous

type
omit_subgoal — designed under this label but realized by premature_serve, which is not a name the assistant is offered. The name that earns credit is omit_subgoal.
the cook decides the dish is finished when it is not, abandons the rest and carries an incomplete plate to the pass
fires when
The force gate, and then a position floor: as many units on the plate as the recipe wants up to and including the item named by assemble(steak), whatever those units are. An early serve is premature by how far along the dish is, not by whether the cook plated in the template's order. An empty plate cannot be served.
Stated progress floor: prep steak x1; at least 1 item already on the plate.
forced block
put the plate in hand, walk to the serving pass and serve. Because the block ends in the serve, it releases at the serve — that is, never before the harm has landed.
as spliced in the verification run: go to the serving pass · serve
harm lands
the plate is served with units missing, counted as units and not as a set of names. The cook gets its hands back on nothing: the block ends in the serve, which ends the episode.
catch window
median 7.0 ticks for premature_serve over the 8 that fired in the verification run (range 5–18); 6 ticks in this case. The assistant must name omit_subgoal inside it; wrong_order, repeat_step count as aliases, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

Preps the wrong thing for the steaknot ruinous

type
mis_prep
the wrong ingredient is fetched and prepared at the station the right one should have used, so the real component is never made
item
steak (the wrong thing scooped or prepared: lettuce)
kwargs {"component": "steak", "wrong": "lettuce"}
fires when
The one moment test is the cook's own reach: its current or next command names the steak or its crate, or it is already holding a raw steak. The steak must not be fully prepped already, and the map must have a lettuce crate. This type waits out every smoke — it is not one of the types whose smoke ban can be narrowed.
forced block
a whole prep block for the lettuce, built out of steps this kitchen allows: fetch it, carry it to a free board or the station the real component would have used, and process it there. If the cook is already holding something, the block parks it first; an orphaned ignition of the real component is rewritten or dropped.
as spliced in the verification run: go to the lettuce source · pick up · go to the empty cutting board · put down · chop
harm lands
the lettuce has been processed. The cook gets its hands back on the block has run out (deviated_block_executed).
catch window
median 23.0 ticks for mis_prep over the 13 that fired in the verification run (range 16–37); 17 ticks in this case. The assistant must name mis_prep inside it; repeat_step, serve_raw count as aliases, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

Stalls after fetch_platenot ruinous

type
forget_next_step

fires when
The anchor is a progress floor: the plate is in the cook's hands. Two standing refusals then apply. And no food may reach smoke inside the freeze: the check looks 40 ticks ahead (the 35-tick stall plus the 5 the cook needs to walk back), because freezing beside food that will smoke is a walk-away, not a forget.
forced block

Walks away from the potato near donenot ruinous

type

item

kwargs {"component": "potato", "loops": 2}
fires when
An anchor floor first (the prep the anchor names is reached and at least as many items are plated). Then the food itself: the potato must be near done — from 6 ticks before it finishes to 20 ticks after, an asymmetric window, and never on food that is already smoking. forced block

The circuit is rotated, reversed and jittered per sub-case from a stable seed so it does not read as a script. If the cook is holding the half-built plate, the block parks it first.

harm lands

catch window

Stalls after prep(steak)not ruinous

type
forget_next_step
the cook loses its plan and stands there doing nothing; there is no trace on the plate, so the only way to catch it is to be watching
fires when
The anchor is a progress floor: the steak is in processing. Two standing refusals then apply. A forget never forgets the serve — the moment is refused whenever every goal item is on the plate, the plate is full, or the cook's own next command is the serve. And no food may reach smoke inside the freeze: the check looks 40 ticks ahead (the 35-tick stall plus the 5 the cook needs to walk back), because freezing beside food that will smoke is a walk-away, not a forget.
forced block
one command — idle 35. The cook stands still for the whole freeze (FORGET_FREEZE_TICKS = 35, sized as the harness's 30-tick latency cap plus the 5 ticks the cook needs to walk back) and then picks its own plan up again.

harm lands

type
repeat_step

fires when

This is a prep repeat — the tomato is fetched and processed a second time, which costs time rather than the dish. It needs a free station to do it in: a block whose walk to an empty board has no destination is refused and the sub-case waits. type

type
forget_next_step

fires when

A forget never forgets the serve — the moment is refused whenever every goal item is on the plate, the plate is full, or the cook's own next command is the serve. 0 · standing request

“Give me a shout the moment the steak is done, would you?”
kind
ready:steak
can be asked
The item must be on the heat and still cooking — asking someone to tell you when food that is already done is done is not a request: no steak still cooking on the heat. A request that becomes ready on the same tick as a forced mistake is made first. Progress floor: prep steak x1. Beyond that the voice slot must be free and quiet, no error takeover may be live, and the serve ceiling must not have closed it.
truth
Computed against the live kitchen at the moment of the ask and again at the moment of the answer — never read from a reference plan. An answer that was true when it was given is not wrong. On the clean scripted trace this anchor the answer was {"item": "steak", "station": "pan", "at": [4, 8], "ticks_left": 13, "status": "cooking", "answer": "13 ticks"}.
judged on
not by content but by timing: the earliest line that unconditionally asserts, in the present tense, that this item is done now, scored 1.0 the tick the food is done and decaying linearly to 0 at the burn horizon; an alert more than 2 ticks early voids the request, and silence scores 0.

1 · mistake

Walks away from the rice near donenot ruinous

type
leave_unattended
the cook walks off to other business while food that is about to be ready sits on a lit station, and the food goes through done, grace, smoke and fire behind its back
item
rice
kwargs {"component": "rice", "loops": 2}
fires when
An anchor floor first (the prep the anchor names is reached and at least as many items are plated). Then the food itself: the rice must be near done — from 6 ticks before it finishes to 20 ticks after, an asymmetric window, and never on food that is already smoking. No other food may be lost by the walk (anything elsewhere that would smoke inside the circuit plus the walk back bars it), and it never fires within 3 ticks of a standing request about the same food — a cook that has just handed the watch to the assistant is not walking away negligently.
forced block
busywork drawn from the recipe itself — every other component's crate, then the plates and the serving pass, each followed by an idle of about eight ticks, the whole circuit walked twice. The circuit is rotated, reversed and jittered per sub-case from a stable seed so it does not read as a script. If the cook is holding the half-built plate, the block parks it first.
as spliced in the verification run: go to the plates · idle 8 · go to the serving pass · idle 8 · go to the steak source · idle 7 · go to the tomato source · idle 9 · go to the lettuce source · idle 7 · go to the tortilla source · idle 7 · go to the plates · idle 8 · go to the tortilla source · idle 8 · go to the lettuce source · idle 10 · go to the tomato source · idle 9 · go to the steak source · idle 8 · go to the serving pass · idle 7
harm lands
a busywork walk has finished and the rice's own food has begun to smoke. The cook gets its hands back on max hold at the smoke (smoke_committed) — the rescue is handed back at once.
catch window
median 29.0 ticks for leave_unattended over the 12 that fired in the verification run (range 5–32); 29 ticks in this case. The assistant must name leave_unattended inside it; forget_next_step, omit_subgoal count as aliases, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

truth

On the clean scripted trace this anchor the answer was {"boards": 3, "empty": 2, "empty_at": [[2, 6], [3, 6]], "answer": "2"}.
judged on

3 · mistake

Plates the tomato before the steakruinous

type
wrong_order
on a dish whose assembly order is strict, the cook plates the right items in the wrong order
item
tomato — the item plated too early, before the steak
kwargs {"a": "steak", "b": "tomato"}
fires when
This dish is order-strict, which is the only kind this type is ever placed on. No unit of the steak — the item that should have gone first — may be finishing on this very tick; then the force gate. Neither the steak nor the tomato may already be correctly plated, and the tomato (the item that will go down too early) must be ready, or be a chopped item the block can make itself. Only the item plated too early has to exist: requiring the steak as well starved every live run.
Stated progress floor: at least 1 item already on the plate.
forced block
the two assembly steps are swapped. If the tomato is a chopped item and no chopped unit exists, the block parks the plate, chops the tomato itself, retrieves the same plate and scoops the tomato before the steak. While the block runs, the steak is held back from smoking.
as spliced in the verification run: go to the empty counter · put down · go to the tomato source · pick up · go to the empty cutting board · put down · chop · go to the counter with the plate · pick up · go to the cutting board with the tomato · scoop
harm lands
some plate build received the tomato before the steak, or holds the tomato with no steak under it. The cook gets its hands back on the whole block has run — deliberately not at the plate commit, because releasing on the first scoop turned the swap into an omission.
catch window
median 40.0 ticks for wrong_order over the 8 that fired in the verification run (range 18–46); 40 ticks in this case. The assistant must name wrong_order inside it; omit_subgoal, repeat_step count as aliases, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

4 · question · Q7

“The thing I put on right after the tortilla — what is it, and is it done?”
kind
after_ref:tortilla — plate ['tortilla', 'rice', 'steak']: the referent is 2 platings back
can be asked
The referent must be on the plate with something plated after it: nothing on the plate after the tortilla yet. Progress floor: prep rice x1, steak x1. Beyond that the voice slot must be free and quiet, no error takeover may be live, and the serve ceiling must not have closed it.
truth
Computed against the live kitchen at the moment of the ask and again at the moment of the answer — never read from a reference plan. An answer that was true when it was given is not wrong. On the clean scripted trace this anchor the answer was {"ref": "tortilla", "referent": "rice", "referent_t": 185, "state": "cooked", "answer": "the rice (cooked)"}.
judged on
the referent must resolve to the right item and its state; the right state on the wrong item is incorrect.

5 · mistake

Does the lettuce prep a second timenot ruinous

type
repeat_step
the cook loses track of having already done a step and goes back to do it again
item
lettuce
kwargs {"sid": "prep(lettuce)", "at": "assemble(tomato)"}
fires when
The at anchor is a progress floor: the tomato must be correctly on the plate — without it a prep repeat fired the tick its component first entered a station, which is a consecutive re-do and not a memory slip. This is a prep repeat — the lettuce is fetched and processed a second time, which costs time rather than the dish. It needs a free station to do it in: a block whose walk to an empty board has no destination is refused and the sub-case waits. The item must also not be one whose second unit reads as legitimate — a second bun is a top bun, not a duplicate.
Stated progress floor: prep lettuce x1; at least 4 items already on the plate.
forced block
the template step is simply run again now. For a chopped item with no second prepared unit left, the block parks the dish plate, makes the duplicate itself, and picks the same plate back up so the duplicate lands on the dish and not on a scratch plate.
as spliced in the verification run: go to the empty counter · put down · go to the lettuce source · pick up · go to the empty cutting board · put down · chop
harm lands
one plate build holds more units of the lettuce than the recipe needs (an assemble repeat), or more units than needed have entered processing (a prep repeat). The cook gets its hands back on the block has run out (deviated_block_executed).
catch window
median 31.5 ticks for repeat_step over the 12 that fired in the verification run (range 2–49); 35 ticks in this case. The assistant must name repeat_step inside it; add_foreign counts as an alias, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

5 · mistake

Carries the plate to the pass with only the tomato (and what came before) on itruinous

type
omit_subgoal — designed under this label but realized by premature_serve, which is not a name the assistant is offered. The name that earns credit is omit_subgoal.
the cook decides the dish is finished when it is not, abandons the rest and carries an incomplete plate to the pass
fires when
The force gate, and then a position floor: as many units on the plate as the recipe wants up to and including the item named by assemble(tomato), whatever those units are. An early serve is premature by how far along the dish is, not by whether the cook plated in the template's order. An empty plate cannot be served.
Stated progress floor: prep rice x1, steak x1; at least 4 items already on the plate.
forced block
put the plate in hand, walk to the serving pass and serve. Because the block ends in the serve, it releases at the serve — that is, never before the harm has landed.
harm lands
the plate is served with units missing, counted as units and not as a set of names. The cook gets its hands back on nothing: the block ends in the serve, which ends the episode.
catch window
median 7.0 ticks for premature_serve over the 8 that fired in the verification run (range 5–18). The assistant must name omit_subgoal inside it; wrong_order, repeat_step count as aliases, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

5 · mistake

An off-recipe item goes into the lettuce's slot once it is preppedruinous

type
add_foreign — designed under this label but realized by misidentify_assemble, which is not a name the assistant is offered. The name that earns credit is add_foreign.
the wrong thing is scooped into the slot where a recipe item should have gone — nothing extra appears, something correct is simply missing and something wrong is in its place
item
lettuce (the wrong thing scooped or prepared: avocado)
kwargs {"component": "lettuce", "wrong": "avocado"}
fires when
No unit of the lettuce may be finishing on this very tick; then the force gate — the dish under way, the plate neither complete nor queued for the pass. The lettuce's own slot must still be open (a correctly plated lettuce means the moment has passed), the plate must have room, and the map must have an avocado crate. Nothing is asked of the cook's own plan: the block fetches the plate itself.
Stated progress floor: prep rice x1, steak x1, lettuce x1; at least 3 items already on the plate.
forced block
the lettuce's own assembly step is replaced by a walk to the avocado crate and a scoop, with the plate fetch inserted ahead of it — typically go to the plates · pick up · go to the avocado source · scoop.
harm lands
a new unit of the avocado reaches the plate after the injection. The cook gets its hands back on max hold, plate_committed.
catch window
median 12.0 ticks for misidentify_assemble over the 13 that fired in the verification run (range 3–30). The assistant must name add_foreign inside it; repeat_step counts as an alias, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

6 · mistake

Does the lettuce prep a second timenot ruinous

type
repeat_step
the cook loses track of having already done a step and goes back to do it again
item
lettuce
kwargs {"sid": "prep(lettuce)", "at": "assemble(tomato)"}
fires when
The at anchor is a progress floor: the tomato must be correctly on the plate — without it a prep repeat fired the tick its component first entered a station, which is a consecutive re-do and not a memory slip. This is a prep repeat — the lettuce is fetched and processed a second time, which costs time rather than the dish. It needs a free station to do it in: a block whose walk to an empty board has no destination is refused and the sub-case waits. The item must also not be one whose second unit reads as legitimate — a second bun is a top bun, not a duplicate.
Stated progress floor: prep lettuce x1; at least 4 items already on the plate.
forced block
the template step is simply run again now. For a chopped item with no second prepared unit left, the block parks the dish plate, makes the duplicate itself, and picks the same plate back up so the duplicate lands on the dish and not on a scratch plate.
harm lands
one plate build holds more units of the lettuce than the recipe needs (an assemble repeat), or more units than needed have entered processing (a prep repeat). The cook gets its hands back on the block has run out (deviated_block_executed).
catch window
median 31.5 ticks for repeat_step over the 12 that fired in the verification run (range 2–49). The assistant must name repeat_step inside it; add_foreign counts as an alias, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

3 · mistake

Stalls after assemble(steak)not ruinous

type
forget_next_step
the cook loses its plan and stands there doing nothing; there is no trace on the plate, so the only way to catch it is to be watching
fires when
The anchor is a progress floor: the plate is through the steak. Two standing refusals then apply. A forget never forgets the serve — the moment is refused whenever every goal item is on the plate, the plate is full, or the cook's own next command is the serve. And no food may reach smoke inside the freeze: the check looks 40 ticks ahead (the 35-tick stall plus the 5 the cook needs to walk back), because freezing beside food that will smoke is a walk-away, not a forget.
forced block
one command — idle 35. The cook stands still for the whole freeze (FORGET_FREEZE_TICKS = 35, sized as the harness's 30-tick latency cap plus the 5 ticks the cook needs to walk back) and then picks its own plan up again.
as spliced in the verification run: idle 35
harm lands
the stall runs to its end. The cook gets its hands back on the block has run out (deviated_block_executed).
catch window
median 37.0 ticks for forget_next_step over the 18 that fired in the verification run (range 37–49); 37 ticks in this case. The assistant must name forget_next_step inside it; repeat_step counts as an alias, and the cook itself may also credit a call it judges correct.

The ten kitchens

Each tier is one dish on two maps. The stations and crates below are the map as the rollout builds it: the engine adds a second cutting board to any kitchen that has one (a single board deadlocked a cook whose board was occupied by a mis-prepped item), and tops the map up to two off-recipe crates, because a mistake that scoops something wrong needs something wrong to scoop and a kitchen with a single distractor made “flag anything with lettuce” a cheap winning policy.

master_nops_hard_mapLoaded Burrito1. tortilla (raw) 2. rice (cooked) 3. steak (cooked) 4. tomato (chopped) 5. lettuce (chopped)cutting board x3, pan x2, pot x2, fryer, oven, plate stack, serving pass, binavocado, bun, cheese, corn, dough, meat, onion, shrimp9x9

The two variants exist because the case-level inject flag in the suite file, which alternates true/false down the list, is not about injecting mistakes at all: it is passed to the map builder, where it controls whether a lettuce decoy crate and a bin are added to a map that lacks them. On eight of the ten kitchens it changes nothing. On hard_nops_hard_map and expert_nops_hard_map the odd-numbered cases get a lettuce crate and the even-numbered ones a cheese crate in its place. No sub-case in the suite names an ingredient its own map lacks — that was checked for all 50 — so nothing is broken by it, but the two maps are not identical across the five cases of their cell.

The verification run

The evidence strip on each card comes from one pass of the whole suite at this engine rev: the baseline cook persona, a real LLM cook, and a silent assistant seat — 4,313 assistant turns with no text in any of them. That is deliberate: it measures what the engine does when nobody helps, which is the only thing a firing check can measure. Outcomes over the 50 episodes: 37 wrong_serve, 6 burned, 4 timeout, 3 won. Sub-cases that fired: 116/140 mistakes, 68/88 questions, 18/29 requests, 3/3 plan changes. Every one of the 116 mistakes that fired also committed, and every skip carries the reason the card quotes. Because the seat is silent, no detection, answer or alert score exists in this run at all; the cards say what the assistant would be graded on, not how any assistant did.
